Lemma 4.2
.

Let
(X,\alpha)
be a
\mathcal{Q}
-category and let
\eta_{(X,\alpha)}\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(P(X,\alpha),\pi)
be the

\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embedding, given by

\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)=(|x|,\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x)),\qquad x\in X_{0}.

Suppose that there exists a functor
\xi\colon(P(X,\alpha),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
, then the following assertions are equivalent
:

(i)

\xi
is left adjoint to
\eta_{(X,\alpha)}
— i.e.,
\alpha(\xi({p},{g}),x)=\pi(({p},{g}),\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))
for all
x\in X_{0}
and
({p},{g})\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}
.

(ii)

1_{|\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))|}\leq\alpha(x,\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))
and
1_{|\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))|}\leq\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),x)
for all
x\in X_{0}
.

(iii)

For all
x\in X_{0}
the following relations hold
:

\alpha(x,x)=\alpha(x,\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))=\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),x).

Proof.

Assume that (i) holds. Then for all
x\in X_{0}
we have:

\displaystyle 1_{|\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))|}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))=\pi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x),\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))))

\displaystyle=\alpha(x,\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))

and

\displaystyle 1_{|\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))|}

\displaystyle=1_{|\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)|}\leq\pi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x),\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))=\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),x).

Hence (ii) is verified.

Conversely, assume that (ii) holds. For any
({p},{g})\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}
and
x\in X_{0}
observe:

\displaystyle g(x)

\displaystyle=\pi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x),({p},{g}))\leq\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),\xi({p},{g}))

\displaystyle\leq\alpha(x,\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))\circ\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),\xi({p},{g}))\leq\alpha(x,\xi({p},{g})),

— i.e.
g\leq\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(\xi({p},{g}))
. An application of this observation leads to the following relation:

\displaystyle\alpha(\xi({p},{g}),x)

\displaystyle=\pi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(\xi({p},{g})),\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))\leq\pi(({p},{g}),\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))\leq\alpha(\xi({p},{g}),\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))

\displaystyle\leq\alpha(\xi({p},{g}),\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)))\circ\alpha(\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x)),x)\leq\alpha(\xi({p},{g}),x)).

Thus, (i) follows.
Finally, since
|x|=|\xi(\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(x))|
for all
x\in X_{0}
, the equivalence (ii)
\iff
(iii) follows from Lemma
4.1
(a).
∎

A
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
is said to be
cocomplete
if the
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embedding

\eta_{(X,\alpha)}\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(P(X,\alpha),\pi)

admits a left adjoint functor
\sup\nolimits_{X}\colon(P(X,\alpha),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
.

If
(X,\alpha)
is separated, then by Lemma
4.1
and Lemma
4.2
, cocompleteness of
(X,\alpha)

is equivalent to the existence of a
functor
\sup\nolimits_{X}\colon(P(X,\alpha),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{\small(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{(X,\alpha)}}
\textstyle{\small(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{1_{(X,\alpha)}}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\sup\nolimits_{X}}

Referring to
[
22
]
, the
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(P(X,\alpha),\pi)
is separated and cocomplete, and serves as the free cocompletion of
(X,\alpha)
.

In fact, the component
\mu_{(X,\alpha)}\colon(P(P(X,\alpha),\pi),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(P(X,\alpha),\pi)

of the multiplication of the contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf monad (cf.
[
13
, Rem. 5.4]
) is given by:

\bigl(\mu_{(X,\alpha)}({p},{G})\bigr)(x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{({q},{g})\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}}g(x)\circ G({q},{g}),\qquad({p},{G})\in P(P(X,\alpha),\pi)_{0},\,x\in X_{0},

and the endofunctor
\mathds{P}\colon\mathtt{Cat}(\mathcal{Q})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathtt{Cat}(\mathcal{Q})
of the contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf monad acts on morphisms
\varphi\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(Y,\beta)
of
\mathtt{Cat}(\mathcal{Q})
as follows:

\bigl(\mathds{P}(\varphi)({p},{g})\bigr)(y)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}\limits_{x\in X_{0}}\beta(y,\varphi(x))\circ{g}(x),\qquad y\in Y_{0},({p},{g})\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}.

This functor
\mu_{(X,\alpha)}
is left adjoint to the
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embedding
\eta_{P(X,\alpha)}
.
Moreover, if
(X,\alpha)
is separated and cocomplete
with
\sup\nolimits_{X}\colon(P(X,\alpha),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
being left adjoint to
\eta_{(X,\alpha)}
,
then the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\sup\nolimits_{X}}
\textstyle{(P(P(X,\alpha),\pi),\pi)}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\mu_{(X,\alpha)}}
\textstyle{(P(P(X,\alpha),\pi),\pi)}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\mathds{P}(\sup\nolimits_{X})}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\sup\nolimits_{X}}

In fact, the repeated application of the left adjointness of
\sup\nolimits_{X}
implies the following relation for all
({p},{G})\in P(P(X,\alpha))
and all
x\in X_{0}
:

\displaystyle\alpha(\sup\nolimits_{X}(\mathds{P}(\sup\nolimits_{X})({p},{G})),x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{({q},{g})\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}}\bigl(G({q},{g})\searrow\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{z\in X_{0}}(\alpha(z,\sup\nolimits_{X}({q},{g}))\searrow\alpha(z,x))\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{({q},{g})\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}}\bigl(G({q},{g})\searrow\alpha(\sup\nolimits_{X}({q},{g}),x)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{({q},{g})\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}}\bigl(G({q},{g})\searrow\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{z\in X_{0}}({g}(z)\searrow\alpha(z,x))\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\alpha(\sup\nolimits_{X}(\mu_{(X,\alpha)}({p},{G})),x).

Since
(X,\alpha)
is separated, the relation
\sup\nolimits_{X}(\mathds{P}(\sup\nolimits_{X})({p},{G}))=\sup\nolimits_{X}(\mu_{(X,\alpha)}({p},{G}))
follows. Hence, separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-categories are
algebras
of the contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf monad (see again
[
13
, Rem. 5.4]
).
Moreover, cocompleteness and completeness are equivalent concepts (cf.
[
22
]
).
Since, analogously to
(P(X,\alpha),\pi)
, the
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha),\upsilon)
of covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaves is separated and complete, it is also cocomplete. However, we now provide a direct proof of this property.

Proposition 4.3
.

The
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha),\upsilon)
is separated and cocomplete.

Proof.

Clearly,
(\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha),\upsilon)
is separated.
To show cocompleteness, consider the functor

\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)}\colon\bigl(P(\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha),\upsilon),\pi\bigr)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha),\upsilon)

defined for all
(p,G)\in P(\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha),\upsilon)_{0}
by:

(4.1)

\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)}(p,G)(x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{({q},{f})\in\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)_{0}}\bigl(G({q},{f})\searrow f(x)\bigr),\qquad x\in X_{0}.

The previous formula defines a covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf of type
p
on
(X,\alpha)
.
Furthermore, if
x\in X_{0}
and
({q},{f})\in\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)_{0}
, then we observe:

G_{1}({q},{f})\circ\pi(({p}_{1},G_{1}),({p}_{2},G_{2}))\circ\bigl(\mathtt{sup}_{X}(p_{2},G_{2})(x)\bigr)\leq f(x),

which implies
\pi(({p}_{1},G_{1}),({p}_{2},G_{2}))\leq\upsilon(({p}_{1},\sup\nolimits_{X}(p_{1},G_{1})),({p}_{2},\sup\nolimits_{X}(p_{2},G_{2})))
.

Hence
\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)}
is a functor.

Finally, using the associativity of the composition in
\mathcal{Q}
, we show that
\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)}
is left adjoint to the
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embedding
\eta_{(\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)),\upsilon)}
. Let
({r},{h})
be another covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf on
(X,\alpha)
. Then the left-adjointness is expressed by:

\displaystyle\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{x\in X_{0}}\bigl(\mathtt{sup}_{X}(p,G)(x)\swarrow h(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{x\in X_{0}}\bigl(\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{({q},{f})\in\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)_{0}}\bigl(G({q},{f})\searrow f(x)\bigr)\bigr)\swarrow h(x)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{({q},{f})\in\mathcal{Q}(X,\alpha)_{0}}\bigl(G({q},{f})\searrow\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{x\in X_{0}}(f(x)\swarrow h(x))\bigr)\bigr).

This completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 4.4
.

The
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathcal{R}^{r},\varrho)
, introduced in Remark
3.2
, is separated and cocomplete. The structure map
\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}^{r}}\colon(P(\mathcal{R}^{r},\varrho),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathcal{R}^{r},\varrho)
has the form
:

(4.2)

\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}^{r}}(p,g)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{{u}\in\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0}}(g({u})\searrow{u}),\qquad(p,g)\in P(\mathcal{R}^{r},\varrho)_{0}.

Let
(X,\alpha)
and
(Y,\beta)
be separated and cocomplete categories, and let
\sup\nolimits_{X}

and
\sup\nolimits_{Y}
be the left adjoint functors of their respective
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embeddings. Then a functor
\varphi\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(Y,\beta)
is cocontinuous if and only if the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(Y,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\sup\nolimits_{X}}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\textstyle{(P(Y,\beta),\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\mathds{P}(\varphi)}
\textstyle{(P(Y,\beta),\pi)}
\textstyle{(Y,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\sup\nolimits_{Y}}

where
\mathds{P}
is the endofunctor of the contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf monad (see above).

An example of a cocontinuous functor is given in the next lemma.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
(X,\alpha)
a separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category. Then for each
x\in X_{0}
with
|x|=r
, the contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x)\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathcal{R}^{r},\varrho)
, viewed as a functor, is cocontinuous.

Proof.

Let
\sup\nolimits_{X}
and
\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}^{r}}
be the left adjoint functors of
the respective
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embeddings.
For all
(p,g)\in P(X,\alpha)_{0}
we compute:

\displaystyle\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}^{r}}(\mathds{P}(\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x))(p,g))

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{\lambda\in\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0}}\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{y\in X_{0}}\bigl((\varrho(\lambda,\alpha(y,x))\circ g(y))\searrow\lambda\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{y\in X_{0}}\bigl(g(y)\searrow\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{\lambda\in\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0}}(\varrho(\lambda,\alpha(y,x))\searrow\lambda)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{y\in X_{0}}(g(y)\searrow\alpha(y,x))=\alpha(\sup\nolimits_{X}(p,g),x).

Hence, the cocontinuity of
\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x)
is verified.
∎

Comment 4.6
.

The previous lemma is a generalization of the well known fact that in complete lattices the characteristic map of the principal down-set
{{\hbox to0.0pt{$\ $\hss}\hbox{$\downarrow$}}}x=\{\,y\in X\mid y\leq x\,\}
is join-preserving.

Let
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
be the category of separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-categories, with cocontinuous functors as morphisms. We claim that the
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
, where
\tau(p,q)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}\text{\rm hom}(q,p)
, is the terminal object of
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
.
The type function of
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
is the
identity, so
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
is separated. Further, for every contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
(p,g)
on
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
, the relation

\tau(p,q)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{r\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}(g(r)\searrow\tau(r,q))
holds. This
means that the type function of

P(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
is left adjoint to the
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embedding of
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
, so
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
is cocomplete and therefore an object of
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
.
Finally, the commutative diagram

\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{|\phantom{x}|}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\sup\nolimits_{X}}
\textstyle{(P(X,\alpha),\pi)}
\textstyle{(P(\mathcal{Q},\tau),\pi)}
\scriptstyle{\mathds{P}(|\phantom{x}|)}
\textstyle{(P(\mathcal{Q},\tau),\pi)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{|\phantom{x}|=\sup\nolimits_{X}}

points out that the type function of every separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
viewed as a functor
|\phantom{x}|\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
is cocontinuous.

Conclusion:
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
is the terminal object in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
.

Proposition 4.7

(Points in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
)
.

Let
(X,\alpha)
be a separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category. A functor
\varphi\colon(\mathcal{Q},\tau)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
is
cocontinuous if and only if
\varphi
satisfies the following additional property for all
x\in X_{0}
and for all
r\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}
:

(4.3)

\alpha\bigl(\varphi(r),x\bigr)=\tau\bigl(r,|x|\bigr).

Proof.

Let
\bot_{qr}
be the universal lower bound of
\text{\rm hom}(q,r)
. Since the composition in
\mathcal{Q}
is join-preserving, we first notice that the relation

(4.4)

\bot_{qr}\circ u\leq v

holds for all
u\in\text{\rm hom}(s,q)
and
v\in\text{\rm hom}(s,r)
, and we define a contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
(q,g)
on
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
by:

g(r)=\bot_{qr},\qquad r\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}.

If
\varphi
is cocontinuous, then we apply (
4.4
) and obtain:

\displaystyle\alpha\bigl(\varphi(q),x\bigr)

\displaystyle=\alpha\bigl(\sup\nolimits_{X}(\mathds{P}(\varphi)(q,g)),x\bigr)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{r\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\bigl(g(r)\searrow\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{z\in X_{0}}\alpha\bigl(z,\varphi(r)\bigr)\searrow\alpha(z,x)\bigr)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{r\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\bot_{qr}\searrow\alpha\bigl(\varphi(r),x\bigr)=\tau(q,|x|).

Conversely, if condition (
4.3
) holds, then for any contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
(p,g)
on
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
and any
x\in X_{0}
we compute:

\displaystyle\alpha\bigl(\sup\nolimits_{X}(\mathds{P}(\varphi)(p,g)),x\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{r\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\bigl(g(r)\searrow\alpha(\varphi(r),x)\bigr)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{r\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\bigl(g(r)\searrow\tau(r,|x|)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\tau(p,|x|)=\alpha(\varphi(p),x).

Since
(X,\alpha)
is separated, it follows that
\varphi\bigl(\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{Q}}(p,g)\big)=\varphi(p)=\sup\nolimits_{X}(\mathds{P}(\varphi)(p,g))
— i.e.,
\varphi
is cocontinuous.
∎

Remark 4.8
.

(1) (Weak subobject classifier) To prepare the definition of the weak subobject classifier (cf.
[
14
]
), we introduce some additional terminology.
Let
r
be an object of
\mathcal{Q}
. Then
\tau(r,r)
is the top element of
\text{\rm hom}(r,r)
.
A morphism
{r}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.13771pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{u}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{q}
is called
right-sided
if
u\circ\tau(r,r)\leq u
.
If
\tau(r,r)=1_{r}
, then every morphism with domain
r
is right-sided.
Further, let
\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})
denote the set of all right-sided morphisms
{u}
with
\text{\rm dom}(u)=r
. Then
\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})\subseteq\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r}
. On
\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})
, we consider the structure of
(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r},\varrho)

restricted to
\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})

— i.e., the type map of
\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})
is the
restriction of the type map of
\mathcal{R}^{r}
, the hom-arrow-assignment is given by

\varrho({u},{v})={u}\swarrow{v},\qquad{u},{v}\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r}),

and the structure map
\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}\colon(P(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)
is defined by

\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}(p,g)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{{u}\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})}(g({u})\searrow{u}),\qquad(p,g)\in P(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)_{0}.

In fact,
\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}(p,g)
is right-sided, since

\sup\nolimits_{\mathcal{R}^{r}}(p,g)\circ\tau(r,r)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{{u}\in\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0}}\bigl(g({u})\searrow({u}\circ\tau(r,r))\bigr)\leq\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}(p,g).

As we will see below, the separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)
will play the role of a weak subobject classifier in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
.

(2) (Arrow true) The map
\tau(\underline{\phantom{x}},r)
defines a functor
\tau(\underline{\phantom{x}},r)\colon(\mathcal{Q},\tau)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)
, and the relation

{\tau(q,|{u}|)\circ{u}}\leq\tau(q,r)
holds for all
q\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}
and
{u}\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0})
. Hence,

\varrho(\tau(q,r),{u})=\tau(q,|{u}|)

follows, and Proposition
4.7
ensures that the functor
\tau(\underline{\phantom{x}},r)

is cocontinuous. This observation motivates the following terminology: The arrow
\tau(\underline{\phantom{x}},r)
is called the
arrow true of type
r
, and is denoted by
\text{\rm true}_{r}
.

Theorem 4.9
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a
r
-stable small quantaloid,
(X,\alpha)
be a separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category, and let
\varphi\colon(\mathcal{Q},\tau)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
be a cocontinuous functor. Then the following diagram is a pullback square in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
:

(4.5)

\textstyle{(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{\text{\rm true}_{r}}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)}
\scriptstyle{\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},\varphi(r))}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{1_{\mathcal{Q}_{0}}}

Proof.

Referring to equation (
2.1
), we first observe:

\alpha(\underline{\,\,\,\,},\varphi(r))=\alpha(\underline{\,\,\,\,},\varphi(r)\circ\alpha(\varphi(r),\varphi(r))=\alpha(\underline{\,\,\,\,},\varphi(r))\circ\tau(r,r)),

which implies that the range of
\alpha(\underline{\,\,\,\,},\varphi(r))
is contained in
\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})
. Consequently, the diagram (
4.5
) is commutative. To verify the universal property, let
(Y,\beta)
be another separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category, and let
\psi\colon(Y,\beta)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
be a cocontinuous functor satisfying the condition:

\alpha(\psi(y),\varphi(r))=\text{\rm true}_{r}(|y|)=\tau(|y|,r),\qquad y\in Y_{0}.

By Proposition
4.7
, we also have
\alpha(\varphi(r),\psi(y))=\tau(r,|\psi(y)|)=\tau(r,|y|)
. Now we consider the compositions:

\tau(|y|,r)\circ\tau(r,|y|)=\alpha(\psi(y),\varphi(r))\circ\alpha(\varphi(r),\varphi(|y|))\leq\alpha(\psi(y),\varphi(|y|))

and

\tau(|y|,r)\circ\tau(r,|y|)=\alpha(\varphi(|y|),\varphi(r))\circ\alpha(\varphi(r),\psi(y))\leq\alpha(\varphi(|y|),\psi(y)).

By the
r
-stability of
\mathcal{Q}
, it follows that
1_{|y|}\leq\alpha(\psi(y),\varphi(|y|))
and
1_{|y|}\leq\alpha(\varphi(|y|,\psi(y))
.
Using the separation axiom (cf. Lemma
4.1
(a)), we conclude
\psi(y)=\varphi(|y|)
, which means the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{(Y,\beta)}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{(Y,\beta)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{|\phantom{x}|}

Thus, the universal property of diagram (
4.5
) is verified.
∎

Theorem 4.10
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a quantaloid, and let
(X,\alpha)
and
(Y,\beta)
be separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-categories.
Further, suppose that
\varphi\colon(Y,\beta)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
and
\chi\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)
are cocontinuous functors that turn the following diagram into a pullback square
:

(4.6)

\textstyle{(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{\text{\rm true}_{r}}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)}
\scriptstyle{\chi}
\textstyle{(X,\alpha)}
\textstyle{(Y,\beta)}
\scriptstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{(Y,\beta)}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{Q},\tau)}
\scriptstyle{|\phantom{x}|}

Then
\chi
has the form
:

(4.7)

\chi(x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\alpha(x,\varphi(y))\circ\tau(|y|,r),\qquad x\in X_{0}.

Proof.

Let
x\in X_{0}
. From the commutativity of (
4.6
) and axiom
(P1)
, we obtain:

\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\alpha(x,\varphi(y))\circ\tau(|y|,r)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\alpha(x,\varphi(y))\circ\chi(\varphi(y))\leq\chi(x).

(1) Now we choose
x\in X_{0}
such that
\chi(x)=\tau(|x|,r)
, and show that
x
is contained in the image of
\varphi
.
To do this, consider the discrete
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\{x\},\delta)
with
\delta(x,x)=1_{|x|}
. Let
(P(\{x\},\delta),\pi)
be the free cocompletion of
(\{x\},\delta)
, and let
\psi\colon(P(\{x\},\delta),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
be the extension of the embedding of
x
into
X_{0}
to a cocontinuous functor — i.e.,
\psi
is given by (cf.
[
17
, Chap. 1. 4.12]
):

\psi(p,g)=\sup\nolimits_{X}(\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x)\circ g(x)),\qquad(p,g)\in P(\{x\},\delta)_{0}.

Since
\chi
is cocontinuous, we compute:

\displaystyle\chi(\psi(p,g))

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{z\in X_{0}}\varrho(\underline{\phantom{x}},\chi(z))\circ\alpha(z,x)\circ g(x)\bigr)=\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}(\varrho(\underline{\phantom{x}},\chi(x))\circ g(x))

\displaystyle=g(x)\searrow\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{{u}\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})}({u}\swarrow\chi(x))\searrow{u}\bigr)=g(x)\searrow\chi(x)

\displaystyle=g(x)\searrow\tau(|x|,r)=\tau(p,r).

Hence we conclude
\chi\circ\psi=\text{\rm true}_{r}\circ|\phantom{x}|
, and by the universal property of the pullback square (
4.6
), there exists a (unique) cocontinuous functor
\vartheta\colon(P(\{x\},\delta),\pi)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(Y,\beta)
such that
\psi=\varphi\circ\vartheta
. In the special case of the contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
(|x|,g_{0})
with
g_{0}(x)=\alpha(x,x)
we obtain:

\psi(|x|,g_{0})=\sup\nolimits_{X}(\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x)\circ\alpha(x,x))=x=\varphi(\vartheta(|x|,g_{0})),

— i.e.,
x
is contained in the image
\varphi(Y)
.

(2) Finally, for every
x\in X_{0}
consider the contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
(r,g_{x})
on
(X,\alpha)
defined by:

g_{x}(z)=\alpha(z,x)\circ\chi(x),\qquad z\in X_{0}.

Then we observe
\bigl(\mathds{P}(\chi)(r,g_{x})\bigr)({u})=\varrho({u},\chi(x))\circ\chi(x)
for all
{u}\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0})
. Since
\chi
is cocontinuous and right-sided, we obtain:

\displaystyle\chi(\sup\nolimits_{X}(r,g_{x}))

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}(\mathds{P}(\chi)(r,g_{x}))=\chi(x)\searrow\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{{u}\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})}(\varrho({u},\chi(x))\searrow{u})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\chi(x)\searrow\chi(x)=\tau(r,r).

Now, from part (1), we conclude that
\sup\nolimits_{X}(r,g_{x})
is contained in the image of
\varphi
— i.e., there exists an element
y_{x}\in Y_{0}
such that
|y_{x}|=r
and
\sup\nolimits_{X}(r,g_{x})=\varphi(y_{x})
. Hence, by property
(P1)
, we obtain for all
z\in X_{0}
:

\displaystyle\chi(z)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\alpha(z,x)\circ\chi(x)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\alpha(z,\sup\nolimits_{X}(r,g_{x}))

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\alpha(z,\varphi(y_{x}))\circ\tau(r,r)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\alpha(z,\varphi(y))\circ\tau(|y|,r).

Thus the assertion is verified.
∎

We can summarize the results of Theorem
4.9
and Theorem
4.10
as follows:
Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a
r
-stable, small quantaloid.
Then the pair
((\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho),\text{\rm true}_{r})

satisfies the
axioms (WS1) and (WS2) of a weak subobject classifier, as defined in
[
14
, Def. 4.1 (b)]
. Therefore, we refer to

((\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho),\text{\rm true}_{r})

as the
weak subobject classifier of type
r
in the category
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
. In this context we recall that a weak subobject classifier is unique up to an isomorphism.

With regard to axiom (WS2) (cf. Theorem
4.10
) a monomorphism
\varphi\colon(Y,\beta)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
is called
r
-classifiable if the diagram (
4.6
) forms a pullback square in the category
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}
. In this case, the cocontinuous functor
\chi
in diagram (
4.6
) is uniquely determined by
\varphi
and is called the
characteristic morphism of
\varphi
. Accordingly, we denote it by
\chi_{\varphi}
.

Finally, axiom (WS1) (cf. Theorem
4.9
) ensures that global points in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
are always
r
-classifiable.

Remark 4.11
.

(1) Let
\mathcal{Q}
be a quantaloid with a single object
e
— i.e.,
\mathcal{Q}_{0}=\{e\}
and
\text{\rm hom}(e,e)=(\mathfrak{{Q}},\ast,1_{e})
is a unital quantale.
Then
\mathcal{Q}
is trivially
e
-stable. The underlying set

\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})
of the weak subobject classifier in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
coincides with the subquantale
\mathds{R}(\mathfrak{{Q}})
of all right-sided elements of the quantale
\mathfrak{{Q}}
.
Since every separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category is a right
\mathfrak{{Q}}
-module in
\mathtt{Sup}
(cf.
[
5
, Sec. 3.3.3]
), the underlying order of
(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)
is the dual order of
\mathds{R}(\mathfrak{{Q}})
, and
the right action on
\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})
is given by (cf.
[
5
, Proof (a) of Thm. 3.3.22]
)

{u}\boxdot\varkappa=\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r})}(\varrho(\underline{\phantom{x}},{u})\ast\varkappa)=\varkappa\searrow{u},\qquad{u}\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}_{0}^{r}),\,\varkappa\in\mathfrak{{Q}}=\text{\rm hom}(e,e).

Thus, we recover the weak subobject classifier of right
\mathfrak{{Q}}
-modules in
\mathtt{Sup}

(cf.
[
14
, Exam. 3 (a), Thm. 4.2]
).

(2) Let
\mathcal{Q}
be an
r
-stable and arbitrary quantaloid. Since separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-categories are right
\mathcal{Q}
-modules
(cf.
[
20
,
23
]
and
[
13
, Def. 5.5 and Rem. 5.6]
), it follows from (1) that
Theorems
4.9
and
4.10
provide a significant generalization of
[
14
, Thm. 4.2]
.

(3)
Let
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
be the quantaloid induced by the quantization
\mathfrak{{Q}}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
of
\boldsymbol{2}
(cf. Example
1.7
).
Unlike in case (1), the set of objects of
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
consists of three distinct elements:
\bot
,
b
and
\top
.
The quantaloid
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
is both
b
-stable and
\top
-stable. Since
1_{b}=\tau(b,b)
and
1_{\top}=\tau(\top,\top)
, the weak subobject classifiers of types
b
and
\top
have the form:

({\mathcal{R}}^{b},\varrho)=\bigl(\text{\rm hom}(b,b)\sqcup\text{\rm hom}(b,\top),\varrho\bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad({\mathcal{R}}^{\top},\varrho)=\bigl(\text{\rm hom}(\top,\top)\sqcup\text{\rm hom}(\top,b),\varrho\bigr),

where
\sqcup
denotes the disjoint union. The right actions
\boxdot_{p}
in separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-categories
(X,\alpha)
are given by:

x\boxdot_{p}\varkappa=\sup\nolimits_{X}(\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x)\circ\varkappa),\qquad x\in X_{0},\,p\in\mathcal{Q}_{0},\,\varkappa\in\text{\rm hom}(p,|x|).

It is therefore straightforward to construct the
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
-enriched functors
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}^{op}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathtt{Sup}
which express the module character of the related weak subobject classifiers.

Since both
\text{\rm true}_{r}
and the type arrow
|\phantom{x}|\colon(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
of a
\mathcal{Q}
-category are cocontinuous,
their composition
\text{\rm true}_{r}\circ|\phantom{x}|
is also cocontinuous. This composition defines the largest contravariant

\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf of type
r
on
(X,\alpha)
.
In particular,
\text{\rm true}_{r}(|x|)
is right-sided for all
x\in X_{0}
.
It is not difficult to show that diagram (
4.6
) is a pullback square
if and only if
\varphi\colon(Y,\beta)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(X,\alpha)
is the equalizer of
(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 17.78894pt\raise 7.6875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\chi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.5744pt\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.47002pt\raise-9.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\text{\rm true}_{r}\circ|\phantom{x}|}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.5744pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\bigl(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho\bigr)
.
Hence,
r
-classifiable
subobjects of
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
are always regular.

Let
(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)\times(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)
be the binary product of
(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)
in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
. Its underlying set and the hom-arrow-assignment are given by:

\displaystyle S=\{\,(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\in\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0})\times\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}_{0})\mid|\lambda_{1}|=|\lambda_{2}|\,\}

\displaystyle(\varrho\times\varrho)((\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}),(\overline{\lambda}_{1},\overline{\lambda}_{2}))=(\lambda_{1}\swarrow\overline{\lambda}_{1})\wedge(\lambda_{2}\swarrow\overline{\lambda}_{2}),\qquad(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}),\,(\overline{\lambda}_{1},\overline{\lambda}_{2})\in S.

Let
\pi_{i}
(for
i\in\{\,1,2\,\}
) be the respective projections.
Then the left adjoint functor of the
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embedding of the product is
determined by (cf.
[
17
, Chap. 2, 1.11 Prop.]
):

\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})\times\mathds{R}(R^{r})}(p,g)=\bigl(\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}\bigl(\mathds{P}(\pi_{1})(p,g)\bigr),\sup\nolimits_{\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r})}\bigl(\mathds{P}(\pi_{2})(p,g)\bigr)\bigr).

Let
\langle\text{\rm true}_{r},\text{\rm true}_{r}\rangle
be the global point
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)\times(\mathds{R}(R^{r}),\varrho)
, which is cocontinuous. Its corresponding characteristic morphism

(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)\times(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.48367pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\chi_{\wedge}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.59308pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 25.18616pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\mathds{R}(\mathcal{R}^{r}),\varrho)

has the form (cf. (
4.7
)):

\chi_{\wedge}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{b\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}}\bigl((\lambda_{1}\swarrow\tau(b,r))\wedge(\lambda_{2}\swarrow\tau(b,r))\bigr)\circ\tau(b,r)=\lambda_{1}\wedge\lambda_{2}.

In analogy with topos theory (cf.
[
14
]
) the binary intersection of
r
-classifiable subobject is again
r
-classifiable. Since this construction can naturally be extended to multiple intersections, it follows that every subobject in
\mathtt{Cat}_{sc}(\mathcal{Q})
admits an
r
-classifiable hull.

5.
The category
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}

The category
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
has
\mathcal{Q}
-categories as objects and
left adjoint distributors
as morphisms.

Remarks 5.1
.

(1) The
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
, constructed in Example
2.2
,
serves as the terminal object in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
.
Indeed, for every
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
, the type map
|{\phantom{x}}|\colon{X_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{Q}_{0}}
induces a left adjoint distributor
\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
defined by
\Phi({p},x)=\tau({p},|x|)
for all
({p},x)\in{\mathcal{Q}_{0}\times X_{0}}
. To verify the uniqueness of
\Phi
, consider another left adjoint distributor
\Phi^{\prime}\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(\mathcal{Q},\tau)
with the corresponding right adjoint distributor
\Psi^{\prime}
.
By axiom
(D1)
we have
\Phi^{\prime}({p},x)\in\text{\rm hom}(|x|,{p})
and
\Psi^{\prime}(x,p)\in\text{\rm hom}({p},|x|)
, which implies
\Phi^{\prime}({p},x)\leq\tau({p},|x|)
and
\Psi^{\prime}(x,p)\leq\tau(|x|,{p})
for all
({p},x)\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}\times X
. Hence, by Proposition
2.3
it follows that
\Phi^{\prime}=\Phi
.

(2) Let
{\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)}
and
\Psi\colon(Y,\beta)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(X,\alpha)
be a pair of adjoint distributors — i.e.,
\Phi\dashv\Psi
, and let
x\in X_{0}
.
Then the triple
\mu_{x}=(\sigma_{x},|x|,\tau_{x})
is a presingleton of
(Y,\beta)
, where

\sigma_{x}(y)=\Psi(x,y)\text{ and }\tau_{x}(y)=\Phi(y,x)
for each
y\in Y_{0}
.
Moreover, it follows immediately that

(5.1)

\alpha(x_{1},x_{2})\leq\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x_{1}},\mu_{x_{2}}),\qquad x_{1},x_{2}\in X_{0}.

Theorem 5.2
.

A left adjoint distributor
\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)
is an epimorphism in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
if and only if its right adjoint distributor
\Psi
satisfies the condition

(5.2)

\beta(y,y)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\Phi(y,x)\circ\Psi(x,y),\qquad y\in Y.

Comment 5.3
.

Since axioms
(D2)
and
(D3)
hold, we infer from (
2.1
) that the condition (
5.2
) is equivalent to the following property:

(
5.2^{\prime}
)

\beta(y_{1},y_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\Phi(y_{1},x)\circ\Psi(x,y_{2})=(\Phi\otimes\Psi)(y_{1},y_{2}),\qquad y_{1},y_{2}\in Y.

Proof.

(Necessity): Let
\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)
be an epimorphism in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
.
We consider the
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathcal{Q}(\widehat{Y},\widehat{\beta}),\upsilon)
of covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaves
on
(\widehat{Y},\widehat{\beta})
and use the notation of Remarks
5.1
(2).
Then for each presingleton
\mu=({f},{p},{g})
of
(Y,\beta)
, we define the covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaves

\widehat{f}_{\mu}
and
\widehat{g}_{\mu}
on
(\widehat{Y},\widehat{\beta})
by

\widehat{f}_{\mu}(\mu^{\prime})=\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{g}_{\mu}(\mu^{\prime})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x})\circ\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x},\mu^{\prime}),\qquad\mu^{\prime}\in\widehat{Y}_{0},

and observe that for each
\mu\in\widehat{Y}_{0}
and
x\in X_{0}
the following properties hold:

(5.3)

\displaystyle\widehat{g}_{\mu}(\mu_{x})=\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x})=\widehat{f}_{\mu}(\mu_{x}),\quad\widehat{g}_{\mu_{x}}(\mu)=\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x},\mu)=\widehat{f}_{\mu_{x}}(\mu),

\displaystyle 1_{|\mu_{x}|}\leq\widehat{f}_{\mu_{x}}(\mu_{x})=\widehat{g}_{\mu_{x}}(\mu_{x}).

Using the isomorphism
\Xi
in (
3.3
), we obtain:

(5.4)

\displaystyle(\Xi\otimes\Phi)(\mu,x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}{f}(y)\circ\tau_{x}(y)=\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x})=\widehat{f}_{\mu}(\mu_{x})=\widehat{g}_{\mu}(\mu_{x}).

Now, define the left adjoint distributors
\Gamma,\Theta\colon(\widehat{Y},\widehat{\beta})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(\mathcal{Q}(\widehat{Y},\widehat{\beta}),\upsilon)
by

\Gamma(({q},{g}),\mu)=\upsilon\bigl(({q},{g}),(|\mu|,\widehat{f}_{\mu})\bigr)\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta(({q},{g}),\mu)=\upsilon(({q},{g}),(|\mu|,\widehat{g}_{\mu})).

From equations (
5.3
) and (
5.4
) we deduce:

\displaystyle(\Gamma\otimes\Xi\otimes\Phi)(({q},{g}),x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in\widehat{Y}_{0}}\upsilon(({q},{g}),(|\mu|,\widehat{f}_{\mu}))\circ\widehat{f}_{\mu}(\mu_{x})={g}(\mu_{x})

\displaystyle=\upsilon(({q},{g}),(|\mu_{x}|,\widehat{f}_{\mu_{x}})=\upsilon(({q},{g}),(|\mu_{x}|,\widehat{g}_{\mu_{x}})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in\widehat{Y}_{0}}\upsilon(({q},{g}),(|\mu|,\widehat{g}_{\mu}))\circ\widehat{g}_{\mu}(\mu_{x})=(\Theta\otimes\Xi\otimes\Phi)(({q},{g}),x).

We have shown
\Gamma\otimes\Xi\otimes\Phi=\Theta\otimes\Xi\otimes\Phi
.
Since
\Phi
is an epimorphism in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
, it follows that
\Gamma\otimes\Xi=\Theta\otimes\Xi
. And because
\Xi
is an isomorphism, we conclude
\Gamma=\Theta
.
Now, using the fact that the
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(\mathcal{Q}(\widehat{Y},\widehat{\beta}),\upsilon)
is separated (or skeletal),
we deduce that
\widehat{f}_{\mu}=\widehat{g}_{\mu}
for all
\mu\in\widehat{Y}_{0}
.
Referring to Example
3.4
we apply (
3.4
) and compute

\displaystyle\beta(y,y)

\displaystyle=\widehat{\beta}(\widetilde{y},\widetilde{y})=\widehat{f}_{\widetilde{y}}(\widetilde{y})=\widehat{g}_{\widetilde{y}}(\widetilde{y})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\widehat{\beta}(\widetilde{y},\mu_{x})\circ\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x},\widetilde{y})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\tau_{x}(y)\circ\sigma_{x}(y)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\Phi(y,x)\circ\Psi(x,y),

for each
y\in Y_{0}
— i.e., condition (
5.2
) is satisfied.

(Sufficiency):
Let
\Gamma_{i}\colon(Y,\beta)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z,\gamma)
be left adjoint distributors (for
i\in\{\,1,2\,\}
), and suppose
\Gamma_{1}\otimes\Phi=\Gamma_{2}\otimes\Phi
. Then, we use (
2.2
),
(D2)
,
(D3)
and (
5.2
), and compute:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1}(z,y)

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{1}(z,y)\circ\beta(y,y)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\Gamma_{1}(z,y)\circ\Phi(y,x)\circ\Psi(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}(\Gamma_{1}\otimes\Phi)(z,x)\circ\Psi(x,y)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}(\Gamma_{2}\otimes\Phi)(z,x)\circ\Psi(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y^{\prime}\in Y_{0}}\Gamma_{2}(z,y^{\prime})\circ\beta(y^{\prime},y)\leq\Gamma_{2}(z,y)

for each
y\in Y
and
z\in Z
. By symmetry, we also obtain
\Gamma_{2}(z,y)\leq\Gamma_{1}(z,y)
, and hence
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2}
. Therefore
\Phi
is an epimorphism in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
.
∎

Comment 5.4
.

The proof of Theorem
5.2
is fundamentally different from the proof of
[
18
, Prop. 3.23]
by Q. Pu and D. Zhang, which applies to the special case of commutative, unital, and divisible quantales. It is also distinct from the proof in the context of frame-valued sets (
[
3
, Prop. 2.8.7]
).

Theorem 5.5
.

A left adjoint distributor
\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)
is an extremal monomorphism in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
if and only if its right adjoint distributor
\Psi
satisfies the condition
:

(5.5)

\Psi(x_{1},y)\circ\Phi(y,x_{2})\leq\alpha(x_{1},x_{2}),\qquad x_{1},x_{2}\in X_{0},\,y\in Y_{0}

Comment 5.6
.

Referring to
(D2)
,
(D3)
and (
2.2
), condition (
5.5
) is equivalent to:

(
5.5^{\prime}
)

\alpha(x_{1},x_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\Psi(x_{1},y)\circ\Phi(y,x_{2})=(\Psi\otimes\Phi)(x_{1},x_{2}),\qquad x_{1},x_{2}\in X_{0}.

Proof.

(Necessity): Let
\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)

be an extremal monomorphism in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
. Referring to Remark
5.1
(2) define:

Z_{0}:=\bigl\{\,\mu\in\widehat{Y}_{0}\mid\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X}\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x})\circ\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x},\mu)\,\bigr\}.

Now consider the restriction of
\widehat{\beta}
to
Z_{0}\times Z_{0}
, and define the following distributors:

\Xi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z,\widehat{\beta}),\ \Upsilon\colon(Z,\widehat{\beta})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(X,\alpha),\ \Gamma\colon(Y,\beta)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z,\widehat{\beta}),\ \Theta\colon(Z,\widehat{\beta})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)

\Xi(\mu,x)=\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x}),\quad\Upsilon(x,\mu)=\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x},\mu),\quad\Gamma(\mu,y)=\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\widetilde{y}),\quad\Theta(y,\mu)=\widehat{\beta}(\widetilde{y},\mu).

It is straightforward to verify that:

\Xi\dashv\Upsilon\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta\dashv\Gamma.

We observe that for each
x\in X_{0}
and
y\in Y_{0}
:

(\Theta\otimes\Xi)(y,x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in Z_{0}}\widehat{\beta}(\widetilde{y},\mu)\circ\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x})=\widehat{\beta}(\widetilde{y},\mu_{x})=\tau_{x}(y)=\Phi(y,x).

Thus we have:
\Phi=\Theta\otimes\Xi
. Analogously, we verify
\Psi=\Upsilon\otimes\Gamma
. Now, by the definition of
Z_{0}
and Theorem
5.2
, the left adjoint distributor
\Xi
is an
epimorphism.
Since
\Phi
is an extremal monomorphism, it follows that
\Xi
must be an isomorphism. Therefore, for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in X_{0}
and
y\in Y_{0}
we compute:

\displaystyle\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}\limits_{y\in Y_{0}}\Psi(x_{1},y)\circ\Phi(y,x_{2})

\displaystyle=\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x_{1}},\mu_{x_{2}})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in Z_{0}}\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x_{1}},\mu)\circ\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x_{2}})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in Z_{0}}\Upsilon(x_{1},\mu)\circ\Xi(\mu,x_{2})=\alpha(x_{1},x_{2}),

which verifies condition (

5.5
′

).

(Sufficiency): Assume a decomposition
\Theta\otimes\Xi=\Phi
and
\Upsilon\otimes\Gamma=\Psi
with:

\Xi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z,\gamma),\ \Upsilon\colon(Z,\gamma)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(X,\alpha),\ \Gamma\colon(Y,\beta)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z,\gamma),\ \Theta\colon(Z,\gamma)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
