1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta),

and adjunctions
\Xi\dashv\Upsilon
and
\Theta\dashv\Gamma
.

We assume that
\Xi
is an epimorphism and aim to show that
\Xi
is an isomorphism.
By Theorem
5.2
, the relation
\gamma(z,z)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\Xi(z,x)\circ\Upsilon(x,z)
holds
for all
z\in Z_{0}
,
which is equivalent to
\gamma(z_{1},z_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\Xi(z_{1},x)\circ\Upsilon(x,z_{2})

(see Comment below of Theorem
5.2
). Thus, it suffices to verify:

(5.6)

\alpha(x_{1},x_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{z\in Z_{0}}\Upsilon(x_{1},z)\circ\Xi(z,x_{2}),\qquad x_{1},x_{2}\in X_{0}.

For all
x_{1},x_{2}\in X_{0}
, we compute:

\displaystyle\alpha(x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{z\in Z_{0}}\Upsilon(x_{1},z)\circ\Xi(z,x_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{z\in Z_{0}}\Upsilon(x_{1},z)\circ\gamma(z,z)\circ\Xi(z,x_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{z\in Z_{0},\,y\in Y_{0}}\Upsilon(x_{1},z)\circ\Gamma(z,y)\circ\Theta(y,z)\circ\Xi(z,x_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}(\Upsilon\otimes\Gamma)(x_{1},y)\circ(\Theta\otimes\Xi)(y,x_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\Psi(x_{1},y)\circ\Phi(y,x_{2}).

Now, applying condition (
5.5
):

\alpha(x_{1},x_{2})\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{z\in Z_{0}}\Upsilon(x_{1},z)\circ\Xi(z,x_{2})\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\Psi(x_{1},y)\circ\Phi(y,x_{2})\leq\alpha(x_{1},x_{2}).

Thus, condition (
5.6
) is verified, and
\Xi
is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 5.7
.

The category
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
is an
(
epi, extremal mono
)
-category.

Proof.

(a) (Existence of the (epi, extremal mono)-factorization)

Let
\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)
be a left adjoint distributor, and let
\Psi\colon(Y,\beta)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(X,\alpha)
be its right adjoint distributor.
Using the notation from Remark
5.1
(2) and the first part of the proof of Theorem
5.5
,
we consider the
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(Z,\widehat{\beta})
, where for each
x\in X_{0}
, the presingleton associated with
x
is
\mu_{x}=(\Psi(x,\underline{\phantom{x}}),|x|,\Phi(\underline{\phantom{x}},x))
. Define

Z_{0}=\bigl\{\,\mu\in\widehat{Y}_{0}\mid\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x})\circ\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{x},\mu)\,\bigr\}.

By definition of
Z_{0}
, the left adjoint distributor
\Xi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z,\widehat{\beta})
defined by
\Xi(\mu,x)=\widehat{\beta}(\mu,\mu_{x})
is an epimorphism (cf. Theorem
5.2
).
With regard to
\Gamma
and
\Theta
(see the first part of the proof of Theorem
5.5
) we now observe:

\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\Gamma(\mu_{1},y)\circ\Theta(y,\mu_{2})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in Y_{0}}\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{1},\widetilde{y})\circ\widehat{\beta}(\widetilde{y},\mu_{2})=\widehat{\beta}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}).

Hence, by Theorem
5.5
,
\Theta
is an extremal monomorphism, and since
\Phi=\Theta\otimes\Xi
, the factorization of
\Phi
into an epimorphism followed by an extremal monomorphism is verified.

(b) (Uniqueness of the (epi, extremal mono)-factorization)

Let
\Xi_{i}\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\lx@newpage\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z_{i},\gamma_{i})
and
\Theta_{i}\colon(Z_{i},\gamma_{i})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta)
be two (epi, extremal mono)-factorization of
\Phi
in
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
(for
i\in\{\,1,2\,\}
). Let
\Upsilon_{i}\colon(Z_{i},\gamma_{i})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(X,\alpha)
be the right adjoint distributor of
\Xi_{i}
, and
\Gamma_{i}\colon(Y,\beta)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z_{i},\gamma_{i})
be the right adjoint distributor of
\Theta_{i}
. We show that
\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{1}\colon(Z_{1},\gamma_{1})\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Z_{2},\gamma_{2})
is an isomorphism, with the inverse
\Xi_{1}\otimes\Upsilon_{2}
.

Since both
\Xi_{1}
and
\Xi_{2}
are epimorphisms, we apply condition (

5.2
′

) and compute:

\displaystyle\Theta_{1}\otimes\Xi_{1}\otimes\Upsilon_{2}\otimes\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{1}

\displaystyle=\Phi\otimes\Upsilon_{2}\otimes\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{1}=\Theta_{2}\otimes\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{2}\otimes\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{1}

\displaystyle=\Theta_{2}\otimes\gamma_{2}\otimes\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{1}=\Phi\otimes\Upsilon_{1}=\Theta_{1}\otimes\Xi_{1}\otimes\Upsilon_{1}=\Theta_{1}\otimes\gamma_{1}.

Since
\Theta_{1}
is an extremal monomorphism, we apply condition (

5.5
′

) and conclude:

(\Xi_{1}\otimes\Upsilon_{2})\otimes(\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{1})=\gamma_{1}.

Analogously we show
(\Xi_{2}\otimes\Upsilon_{1})\otimes(\Xi_{1}\otimes\Upsilon_{2})=\gamma_{2}
. Hence, the uniqueness of the
(epi, extremal mono)-factorization is verified.
∎

6.
The symmetry axiom and the Cauchy completion

A
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
is said to be
Cauchy complete
if, for every presingleton
\mu=({f},{p},{g})
, there exists a unique element
x\in X_{0}
such that
\mu=\widetilde{x}
— i.e.,
|x|={p}
,
{f}=\alpha(x,\underline{\phantom{x}})
and
{g}=\alpha(\underline{\phantom{x}},x)
(cf. Example
3.4
). In particular, every Cauchy complete
\mathcal{Q}
-category is separated.

If
(\mathcal{Q},j)
is an involutive quantaloid, then a
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
is Cauchy complete if and only if its dual
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha^{op})
is Cauchy complete.
Moreover, every separated and cocomplete
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
is Cauchy complete.
In fact, if
\sup\nolimits_{X}
is the left adjoint of the
\mathcal{Q}
-enriched Yoneda embedding
\eta_{(X,\alpha)}
,
then applying (
3.1
) yields, for every presingleton
\mu=({f},{p},{g})
of
(X,\alpha)
and every
y\in X_{0}
:

\alpha(\sup\nolimits_{X}({p},{g}),y)=\pi(({p},{g}),\eta_{(X,\alpha)}(y))=\mathop{\textstyle\bigwedge}_{x\in X}(g(x)\searrow\alpha(x,y))=f(y).

Hence, it follows that
\mu=\widetilde{\sup\nolimits_{X}({p},{g})}
. The uniqueness of
\sup\nolimits_{X}({p},{g})
is guaranteed by the separation axiom.

Lemma 6.1
.

If
(X,\alpha)
is a
\mathcal{Q}
-category, then its presingleton space
(\widehat{X},\widehat{\alpha})
is Cauchy complete.

Proof.

We first show that
(\widehat{X},\widehat{\alpha})
is separated.
Let
\mu=({f},{p},{g})
and
\mu^{\prime}=({f}^{\prime},{p},{g}^{\prime})
be presingletons such that
|\mu|={p}=|\mu^{\prime}|
and assume:

\widehat{\alpha}(\mu,\mu)=\widehat{\alpha}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{\alpha}(\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})=\widehat{\alpha}(\mu^{\prime},\mu).

Then we conclude from (
2.1
) and (
3.4
) that the following relation holds:

{g}(x)=\widehat{\alpha}(\widetilde{x},\mu)\circ\widehat{\alpha}(\mu,\mu)=\widehat{\alpha}(\widetilde{x},\mu)\circ\widehat{\alpha}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\leq\widehat{\alpha}(\widetilde{x},\mu^{\prime})={g}^{\prime}(x).

By symmetry, we obtain
{g}^{\prime}\leq{g}
, and hence
\mu=\mu^{\prime}
by (
3.1
).

Now, let
\widehat{\mu}=(\widehat{f},{p},\widehat{g})
be a presingleton of
(\widehat{X},\widehat{\alpha})
. Define a covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
{f}_{0}
and a contravariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
{g}_{0}
on
(X,\alpha)
as follows:

{f}_{0}(x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in\widehat{X}_{0}}\widehat{f}(\mu)\circ{f}(x)\quad\text{and}\quad{g}_{0}(x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in\widehat{X}_{0}}{g}(x)\circ\widehat{g}(\mu),\qquad x\in X.

We show that
{g}_{0}
is left adjoint to
{f}_{0}
. In fact, the following relations hold:

\displaystyle\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}{f}_{0}(x)\circ{g}_{0}(x)

\displaystyle\geq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0},\,\mu\in\widehat{X}_{0}}\widehat{f}(\mu)\circ{f}(x)\circ{g}(x)\circ\widehat{g}(\mu)\geq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu\in\widehat{X}_{0}}\widehat{f}(\mu)\circ 1_{|\mu|}\circ\widehat{g}(\mu)\geq 1_{{p}}

\displaystyle{g}_{0}(x_{1})\circ{f}_{0}(x_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\widehat{X}_{0}}{g}(x_{1})\circ\widehat{g}(\mu)\circ\widehat{f}(\mu^{\prime})\circ{f}^{\prime}(x_{2})\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\widehat{X}_{0}}{g}(x_{1})\circ\widehat{\alpha}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\circ{f}^{\prime}(x_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\widehat{X}_{0}}\widehat{\alpha}(\widetilde{x_{1}},\mu)\circ\widehat{\alpha}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\circ\widehat{\alpha}(\mu^{\prime},\widetilde{x_{2}})\leq\alpha(x_{1},x_{2}).

Hence
\mu_{0}:=({f}_{0},{p},{g}_{0})
is a presingleton of
(X,\alpha)
.

Finally, we show that
\widehat{\mu}=\widetilde{\mu_{0}}
— i.e.,
\widehat{f}=\widehat{\alpha}(\mu_{0},\underline{\phantom{x}})
and
\widehat{g}=\widehat{\alpha}(\underline{\phantom{x}},\mu_{0})
. For arbitrary presingletons
\mu=({f},|\mu|,{g})
and
\mu^{\prime}=({f}^{\prime},|\mu^{\prime}|,{g}^{\prime})
of
(X,\alpha)
we compute:

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}(\mu_{0},\mu)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}{f}_{0}(x)\circ{g}(x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu^{\prime}\in\widehat{X}_{0}}\widehat{f}(\mu^{\prime})\circ{f}^{\prime}(x)\bigr)\circ{g}(x)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{\mu^{\prime}\in\widehat{X}_{0}}\widehat{f}(\mu^{\prime})\circ\widehat{\alpha}(\mu^{\prime},\mu)\leq\widehat{f}(\mu).

Analogously, we show
\widehat{\alpha}(\mu,\mu_{0})\leq\widehat{g}(\mu)
for all
\mu\in\widehat{X}_{0}
. Hence Proposition
2.3
implies
\widehat{f}=\widehat{\alpha}(\mu_{0},\underline{\phantom{x}})
and
\widehat{g}=\widehat{\alpha}(\underline{\phantom{x}},\mu_{0})
.
∎

Because of Lemma
6.1
, the presingleton space of a
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
is also referred to as its
Cauchy completion
.
With regard to the distributor defined in
(\ref{n3.1AA})
, it is easily seen that
(X,\alpha)
is always isomorphic to its Cauchy completion in the sense of
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
.
Consequently,
\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
is equivalent to the subcategory
C\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
, consisting of Cauchy complete
\mathcal{Q}
-categories with functors as morphisms.
In fact, the embedding functor
\mathcal{E}\colon\text{$C\mathcal{Q}$-$\mathtt{Set}$}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\text{$\mathcal{Q}$-$\mathtt{Set}$}
is given by (cf.
[
22
, Prop. 7.14]
):

\mathcal{E}(X,\alpha)=(X,\alpha),\quad(X,\alpha)\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.46458pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.09445pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.1889pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(Y,\beta),\quad\mathcal{E}(\mathcal{\varphi})=\beta(\underline{\,\,\,},\varphi(\underline{\phantom{x}}))\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta).

The left adjoint functor
\mathcal{F}\colon\text{$\mathcal{Q}$-$\mathtt{Set}$}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\text{C$\mathcal{Q}$-$\mathtt{Set}$}
of
\mathcal{E}
has the form:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(X,\alpha)=(\widehat{X},\widehat{\alpha}),\quad\Phi\colon(X,\alpha)\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.06456pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.62848pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\circ}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 13.22636pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces(Y,\beta),\quad(\widehat{X},\widehat{\alpha})\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.48212pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathcal{F}(\Phi)}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.04118pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.08237pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>(\widehat{Y},\widehat{\beta}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\Phi)\bigl(\sigma,p,\tau)=(\overline{\sigma},p,\overline{\tau}\bigr),\quad\overline{\sigma}(y)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\sigma(x)\circ\Psi(x,y),\quad\overline{\tau}(y)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}\Phi(y,x)\circ\tau(x),

where
\Psi
is the right adjoint distributor to
\Phi
. With regard to Corollary
5.7
we come to the conclusion that
C\mathcal{Q}
-
\mathtt{Set}
is an (epi, extremal mono)-category.

If
(\mathcal{Q},j)
is an
involutive
quantaloid, then the symmetry axiom of a
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
can be expressed as follows:

(C4)

\alpha(x,y)=j(\alpha(y,x)),\qquad x,y,\in X
. (Symmetry)

Obviously,
(X,\alpha)
is symmetric if and only if
(X,\alpha)
coincides with
its dual
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha^{op})
(cf. Remark
3.3
)

On this background we introduce the following terminology. The Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom if the Cauchy completion of a symmetric
\mathcal{Q}
-category is again symmetric. It follows immediately from the hom-arrow-assignment of the presingleton space and the symmetry axiom
(C4)
that the
Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom if and only if every presingleton

({f},{p},{g})
is a
singleton
— i.e.,
g=j\circ{f}
(cf.
[
12
, Def. 6.4]
). However, there exist simple examples of involutive quantaloids for which the Cauchy completion does not always preserve the symmetry axiom.

Example 6.2
.

Let
G_{3}=\{\,e,a,b\,\}
be the cyclic group with unity
e
. This group induces a unital and commutative quantale
\mathfrak{{Q}}_{5}
with 5 elements. The Hasse diagram and the multiplication table are given as follows:

We view
\mathfrak{{Q}}_{5}
as a quantaloid with one object, where the type function is trivial and the identity acts as the involution
j
.

Now we consider the discrete
\mathfrak{{Q}}_{5}
-category on
X=\{\,a,b\,\}
— i.e.
\alpha(a,b)=\alpha(b,a)=\bot
and
\alpha(a,a)=\alpha(b,b)=e
. Then we introduce the following pair of

\mathfrak{{Q}}_{5}
-presheaves on
(X,\alpha)
:

{f}(a)=a,\quad{f}(b)=\bot,\quad{g}(a)=b,\quad{g}(b)=\bot,

Since the following relations hold:

e=({f}(a)\ast{g}(a))\vee({f}(b)\ast{g}(b))\quad\text{and}\quad{g}(x)\ast{f}(y)\leq\alpha(x,y),\qquad x,y\in\{a,b\}.

the axioms
(P2)
and
(P3)
are satisfied, and consequently
\mu=({f},|\mu|,{g})
is a presingleton of
(X,\alpha)
. However, since
{f}\neq{g}
, the Cauchy completion of
(X,\alpha)
is not symmetric.

Motivated by the previous example we provide a characterization of the property that the Cauchy completion w.r.t. a given involutive quantaloid preserves the symmetry axiom. This result extends
[
11
, Prop. 3.1]
and addresses a problem posed in
[
1
]
.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
(\mathcal{Q},j)
be a small involutive quantaloid. Then the following statements are equivalent
:

(1)

The Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom of
\mathcal{Q}
-categories.

(2)

For every system
({p_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.47307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{u_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{p_{0}},{p_{0}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.73236pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{v_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{p_{i}})_{i\in I}
of morphisms in
\mathcal{Q}
, satisfying the following properties
:

(a)

1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{u}_{i}\circ{v}_{i}
,

(b)

for all
i_{1},i_{2}\in I
:

\begin{array}[]{rl}{u}_{i_{1}}\circ{v}_{i_{1}}\circ{u}_{i_{2}}\leq{u}_{i_{2}},&{v}_{i_{1}}\circ{u}_{i_{2}}\circ{v}_{i_{2}}\leq{v}_{i_{1}}\\
j({v}_{i_{1}})\circ{v}_{i_{1}}\circ{u}_{i_{2}}\leq j({v}_{i_{2}}),&{v}_{i_{1}}\circ{u}_{i_{2}}\circ j({u}_{i_{2}})\leq j({u}_{i_{1}}),\end{array}

the following relations hold
:

1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{u}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{p}_{i}}}j({u}_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad 1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}j({v}_{i})\mathbin{\circ_{{p}_{i}}}{v}_{i}.

Proof.

(1)
\Longrightarrow
(2):
Let
I
be a typed set with
|i|={p}_{i}\in\mathcal{Q}_{0}
for each
i\in I
. From the system
({p_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.47307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{u_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{p_{0}},{p_{0}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.73236pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{v_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{p_{i}})_{i\in I}
, we construct a symmetric
\mathcal{Q}
-category
\mathds{I}=(I,\alpha_{I})
by defining:

\alpha_{I}({i_{1}},{i_{2}})=({u}_{i_{1}}\searrow{u}_{i_{2}})\wedge({v}_{i_{1}}\swarrow{v}_{i_{2}})\wedge(j({v}_{i_{1}})\searrow j({v}_{i_{2}}))\wedge(j({u}_{i_{1}})\swarrow j({u}_{i_{2}})),

for each
i_{1},i_{2}\in I
.
Then property (b) implies:

(6.1)

{v}_{i_{1}}\circ{u}_{i_{2}}\leq\alpha_{I}({i_{1}},{i_{2}}),\qquad{i_{1}},{i_{2}}\in I.

Now define a covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaf
{f}
and a contravariant
{g}
of type
{p}_{0}
on
\mathds{I}
by:

{f}(i)={u}_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad{g}(i)={v}_{i},\qquad i\in I.

By property (a) and inequality (
6.1
),
{f}
is right adjoint to
{g}
— i.e.,
({f},{p}_{0},{g})
is a presingleton of
\mathds{I}
. Since the Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom, it follows that
{g}=j\circ{f}
. Thus, condition (2) follows.

(2)
\Longrightarrow
(1): Let
(X,\alpha)
be a symmetric
\mathcal{Q}
-category with type map
t\colon X_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathcal{Q}_{0}
, and let
({f},{p}_{0},{g})
be a presingleton of
(X,\alpha)
. Then, by axioms
(P2)
and
(P3)
, the following properties hold for all
x,y\in X_{0}
:

1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}{f}(x)\circ{g}(x)\quad\text{and}\quad{g}(x)\circ{f}(y)\leq\alpha(x,y).

Now, applying the extensionality of contravariant and covariant
\mathcal{Q}
-presheaves (cf.
(P1)
and
(Q1)
) we obtain:

\displaystyle{f}(x)\circ{g}(x)\circ{f}(y)\leq{f}(x)\circ\alpha(x,y)\leq{f}(y),

\displaystyle{g}(x)\circ{f}(y)\circ{g}(y)\leq\alpha(x,y)\circ{g}(y)\leq{g}(x).

Using the symmetry axiom
(C4)
, and again the extensionality, we derive:

(6.2)

\displaystyle{g}(x)\circ{f}(y)\circ j({f}(y))\leq\alpha(x,y)\circ j({f}(y))=j({f}(y)\circ\alpha(y,x))\leq j({f}(x))

\displaystyle j({g}(x))\circ{g}(x)\circ{f}(y)\leq j({g}(x))\circ\alpha(x,y)=j(\alpha(y,x)\circ{g}(x))\leq j({g}(y)).

Thus, the system
({t(x)}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.26733pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{{f}(x)}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{{p}_{0}},{{p}_{0}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.68924pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{{g}(x)}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 31.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{t(x)})_{x\in X_{0}}

satisfies the hypothesis of condition (2) — i.e., (a) and (b). Therefore, by application of (2) the relations:

1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}{f}(x)\circ j({f}(x))\quad\text{and}\quad 1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}j({g}(x))\circ{g}(x)

follow. Now we refer to (
6.2
) and obtain for each
x,y\in X_{0}
:

\displaystyle{g}(x)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{y\in X_{0}}{g}(x)\circ{f}(y)\circ j({f}(y))\leq j({f}(x)),

\displaystyle{f}(y)\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{x\in X_{0}}j({g}(x))\circ{g}(x)\circ{f}(y)\leq j({g}(y)).

We conclude that

{f}=j\circ{g}
— i.e., the Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom.
∎

Corollary 6.4
.

Let
(\mathcal{Q},j)
be a small involutive quantaloid such that for every system of morphisms
({p_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.47307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{u_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{p_{0}},{p_{0}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.73236pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.40277pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{v_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{p_{i}})_{i\in I}
in
\mathcal{Q}
satisfying the condition
:

1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{u}_{i}\circ{v}_{i},

the following additional conditions also hold
:

1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{u}_{i}\circ j({u}_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad 1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}j({v}_{i})\circ{v}_{i}.

Then the Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom of
\mathcal{Q}
-categories.

Remark 6.5
.

It is easily seen that in the quantaloid
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
in Example
1.7
the condition
1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{u}_{i}\circ{v}_{i}
already implies
1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{u}_{i}\circ j({u}_{i})

and
1_{{p}_{0}}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}j({v}_{i})\circ{v}_{i}
.
Thus, by Corollary
6.4
, the Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom of
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
-categories.

As an illustration of this situation, consider the discrete
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
-category
(\{\cdot\},\delta)
with
|\cdot|=b
and
\delta(\cdot,\cdot)=1_{b}=b
. Its Cauchy completion
(\widehat{\{\cdot\}},\widehat{\delta})
has three presingletons:

–

\mu_{1}=(f_{1},\top,g_{1})
, with
f_{1}(\cdot)=a_{\ell}
and
g_{1}(\cdot)=a_{r}
,

–

\mu_{2}=(f_{2},b,g_{2})
, with
f_{2}(\cdot)=b
and
g_{2}(\cdot)=b
,

–

\mu_{3}=(f_{3},\bot,g_{3})
, with
f_{3}(\cdot)=\bot
and
g_{3}(\cdot)=\bot
.

Since
g_{i}=j\circ f_{i}
for all
i\in\{\,1,2,3\,\}
, the Cauchy completion
(\widehat{\{\cdot\}},\widehat{\delta})
is symmetric, as expected, and is isomorphic to the terminal object of
\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}}
-
\mathtt{Set}

(cf. Remark
5.1
(1)). In fact, the hom-arrow-assignment of the terminal object in
(\mathcal{Q}_{\boldsymbol{2}},\tau)
(cf. Example
2.2
) is:

\tau(\top,\top)=\top,\quad\tau(b,b)=b,\quad\tau(\top,b)=a_{\ell},\quad\tau(b,\top)=a_{r},\,\,\,\tau(\bot,\underline{\phantom{x}})=\tau(\underline{\phantom{x}},\bot)=\bot.

Referring now to Remark
1.4
we have the following:

Corollary 6.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{Q}=(\mathfrak{Q},\ast)
be a commutative quantale, and let
(D\mathfrak{Q},j)
be the induced involutive quantaloid as described in Remark
1.4
.
Then the Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom of
D\mathfrak{Q}
-categories
if and only if the following symmetry property holds
:

{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}({a_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 5.41869pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 5.32262pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\mu_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a_{i}})

for every system
({a_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.41869pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a},{a}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.32262pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\mu_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a_{i}})_{i\in I}
of morphisms in the quantaloid
D\mathfrak{Q}

satisfying the property
{{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{\lambda}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}{\mu}_{i}}
and the following conditions for all

:

\displaystyle{\lambda}_{i_{1}}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i_{1}}}}{\mu}_{i_{1}}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}}}{\lambda}_{i_{2}}\leq{\lambda}_{i_{2}},\quad{\mu}_{i_{1}}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}}}{\lambda}_{i_{2}}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i_{2}}}}{\mu}_{i_{2}}\leq{\mu}_{i_{1}},

1.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a_{i_{1}}}).

Remark 6.7
.

In the case of the involutive quantaloid
(D\mathfrak{Q},j)
in Example
1.6
, the condition
{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{\lambda}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}{\mu}_{i}
already implies the conditions

{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}({a_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.41869pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a})\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}({a}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.41869pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a_{i}})\quad\text{and}\quad{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}({a_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern Therefore, by Corollary
6.6
,
the Cauchy completion preserves the symmetry axiom of
D\mathfrak{Q}
-categories.

Corollary 6.8
.

Let
\mathfrak{Q}=(\mathfrak{Q},\ast,\top)
be an integral and commutative quantale satisfying the following additional property
:

(6.3)

{a}\ast{b}\leq({a}\ast{a})\vee({b}\ast{b}),\qquad{a},{b}\in\mathfrak{Q}.

Then the Cauchy completion with respect to the involutive quantaloid
(D\mathfrak{Q},j)
preserves the symmetry axiom.

Proof.

For all
{\lambda},{\mu}\in\text{\rm hom}({a},{a})
we first refer to (
1.1
) and apply (
6.3
):

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\lambda}\mathbin{\circ_{a}}{\mu}

\displaystyle={a}\ast({a}\rightarrow{\lambda})\ast({a}\rightarrow{\mu})

\displaystyle\leq{a}\ast((({a}\rightarrow{\lambda})\ast({a}\rightarrow{\lambda}))\vee(({a}\rightarrow{\mu})\ast({a}\rightarrow{\mu})))=({\lambda}\mathbin{\circ_{a}}{\lambda})\vee(\mu\mathbin{\circ_{a}}{\mu}).

Now, with regard to Corollary
6.4
, we consider a family
\{({\lambda}_{i},{\mu}_{i})\mid i\in I\}
of pairs
({\lambda}_{i},{\mu}_{i})
of morphisms such that
{\mu}_{i}\in\text{\rm hom}({a},{a}_{i})
and
{\lambda}_{i}\in\text{\rm hom}({a}_{i},{a})
for each
i\in I
and the property
{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}{\lambda}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}{\mu}_{i}
holds. An application of (
6.4
) leads the following relation:

(6.5)

{a}={a}\mathbin{\circ_{a}}{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i_{1},i_{2}\in I}({\lambda}_{i_{1}}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i_{1}}}}{\mu}_{i_{1}})\mathbin{\circ_{a}}({\lambda}_{i_{2}}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i_{2}}}}{\mu}_{i_{2}})\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}({\lambda}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}{\mu}_{i})\mathbin{\circ_{a}}({\lambda}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}{\mu}_{i}).

Using the commutativity and integrality of
\mathfrak{Q}
, we obtain
{a}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.41869pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a_{i}}\in\text{\rm hom}(a,a_{i})
and observe

({\lambda}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}{\mu}_{i})\mathbin{\circ_{a}}({\lambda}_{i}\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}{\mu}_{i})\leq({a}_{i}\rightarrow{\lambda}_{i})\ast{\lambda}_{i}=({a}_{i},{\lambda}_{i},{a})\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}({a},{\lambda}_{i},{a}_{i}).

We apply the previous relation to (
6.5
). Hence
{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}({a_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.41869pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a})\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}({a}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.41869pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.72223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a_{i}})

follows.
Analogously we verify
{a}\leq\mathop{\textstyle\bigvee}_{i\in I}({a_{i}}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.32262pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\mu_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a})\mathbin{\circ_{{a}_{i}}}({a}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.32262pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\mu_{i}}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 19.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{a_{i}})
. Thus, by Corollary
6.4
, the Cauchy completion w.r.t. the involutive quantaloid
(D\mathfrak{Q},j)
preserves the symmetry axiom.
∎

Remarks 6.9
.

(1) Since condition (
6.3
) is trivially satisfied in any linearly ordered quantale, Corollary
6.8

applies to all integral and commutative quantales on
[0,1]
— i.e., to all left-continuous
t
-norms (cf.
[
15
, Ex. 2.4.5]
).

(2) If a unital and commutative quantale
\mathfrak{Q}
is divisible — meaning that for every pair
({a},{b})\in\mathfrak{Q}\times\mathfrak{Q}
with
{a}\leq{b}
, there exists
{c}\in\mathfrak{Q}
such that
{a}={b}\ast{c}
— then
\mathfrak{Q}
is necessarily integral, and condition (
6.3
) holds (cf.
[
10
, Prop. 2.6]
). Therefore, Corollary
6.8
also applies to all unital, commutative, and divisible quantales (cf.
[
18
, Prop. 5.4]
).

Finally, since the involution
j
of an involutive quantaloid
(\mathcal{Q},j)
acts as identity on objects, the symmetrization of a
\mathcal{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
always exists (cf.
[
9
, p. 279]
) and is given by
(X,\alpha\wedge\alpha^{op}))
, where:

(\alpha\wedge\alpha^{op})(x,y)=\alpha(x,y)\wedge j(\alpha(y,x)),\qquad x,y\in X_{0}.

In this context we recall the following important fact (cf.
[
12
, Prop. 6.9]
).

Proposition 6.10
.

If the involutive quantaloid
(\mathcal{Q},j)
preserves the symmetry axiom, then the symmetrization of a Cauchy complete
\mathcal{Q}
-category is again Cauchy complete.

7.
Quantale-valued preorders and quantaloid enriched categories

Let
\mathfrak{Q}=(\mathfrak{Q},\ast,\mbox{}^{\prime})
be an involutive quantale, and let
(D\mathfrak{Q},j)
be the involutive quantaloid associated with
\mathfrak{Q}
by means of diagonal arrows (cf. Remark
1.4
). Every morphism
{a}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.08333pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.47223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{b}
of
D\mathfrak{Q}
corresponds to a unique element
\lambda\in\mathfrak{Q}
. Hence every hom-arrow-assignment
\alpha
of a
D\mathfrak{Q}
-category
(X,\alpha)
induces a map
\psi\colon X_{0}\times X_{0}\>\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 4.49588pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 14.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}\>\mathfrak{Q}
such that
\alpha(x,y)={|y|}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.14351pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\psi(x,y)}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 39.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 39.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{|x|}
for all
x,y\in X_{0}
.
In the following we investigate the properties of
\psi
. Since the composition in
D\mathfrak{Q}
is given by

({a}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 5.08333pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-3.47223pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\lambda}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces{b})\mathbin{\circ_{a}}({c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 4.98726pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.18056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\textstyle{\mu}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 15.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
